 1925ea Experience suggests that a common cause of i.e. 1007
 19261f is unreliable memory or other hardware.  The 1007 assertion
 19265b just happens to cross-check the results of huge numbers of
 192696 memory reads/writes, and so acts (unintendedly) as a stress
 1926d2 test of your memory system.
 1926ef I suggest the following: try compressing the file again,
 192728 possibly monitoring progress in detail with the -vv flag.
 192763 * If the error cannot be reproduced, and/or happens at different
 1927a4   points in compression, you may have a flaky memory system.
 1927e1   Try a memory-test program.  I have used Memtest86
 192815   (www.memtest86.com).  At the time of writing it is free (GPLd).
 192857   Memtest86 tests memory much more thorougly than your BIOSs
 192894   power-on test, and may find failures that the BIOS doesn't.
 1928d3 * If the error can be repeatably reproduced, this is a bug in
 192911   bzip2, and I would very much like to hear about it.  Please
 19294f   let me know, and, ideally, save a copy of the file causing the
 192990   problem -- without which I will be unable to investigate it.
 1929da bzip2/libbzip2: internal error number %d.
 192a04 This is a bug in bzip2/libbzip2, %s.
 192a29 Please report it to me at: jseward@acm.org.  If this happened
 192a67 when you were using some program which uses libbzip2 as a
 192aa1 component, you should also report this bug to the author(s)
 192add of that program.  Please make an effort to report this bug;
 192b19 timely and accurate bug reports eventually lead to higher
 192b53 quality software.  Thanks.  Julian Seward, 30 December 2001.
